It repurposes the company's CIS process portfolio for visible-light communications PDs, and it is **a foundry's hedge: not going all-in on silicon photonics CPO while keeping a foot on the MicroLED path as well**."* ⚠ TAM pequeno (TrendForce: CPO com MicroLED começando a se formar em 2028 e chegando a US$850M em 2030) — registro como opção, não como receita |

**Post-print synthesis:** the official call transcript confirms the print-day reads — FY26 capex **firmly US$60-64bn** (was flagged "reportedly" at print time, now hard), the additional **US$100bn Arizona (→US$265bn)** commitment, and the agentic-AI-CPU-resurgence framing. Fubon's post-print note is the first sell-side model refresh: **FY26/27 EPS lifted to NT$108.80/155.41** (market-high, well above cons NT$100.13/129.26), PT held at NT$3,500, with fresh capacity marks (N3 210-220k / N2 120k end-2027) and two cross-reads — **positive for INTC EMIB-T and MediaTek's ASIC share** on the CoWoS-shortage dynamic. **07-17 hardens the bull consensus**: the Jefferies/Fubon expert call quantifies the two-sided story — near-term GM dilution (N2 ramp, market wanted 68-70%) but **2027 wafer hikes ≥10% + capex $80bn(27)/$95bn(28) + server-CPU strength**, with the CoWoS crunch a read-through tailwind for OSATs (ASE/Amkor) and Intel IBP; **Barclays raises PT to $650** (AI 20%→25% of group '26→'27, $221bn cumulative capex '26-28); Cristina Titu's framing captures the tape's risk — even a clean beat may not clear "euphoric expectations" (the ASML-reaction analog). Net: demand/capex/pricing all revised UP, the only live debate is near-term margin + whether an already-run stock re-rates on a beat. **07-16/20 (further post-print reads):** JPM's post-earnings note (Gokul) is the cleanest bull refresh — rev guide **"40%+ and could end higher,"** **2026 capex ~$62bn midpoint** (→~$78bn/$84bn '27/'28 + a $100bn US add ~2030-31), advanced packaging **~10%→low-teens of rev**, CoWoS numbers **revised up ~3-4×**, GM staying **high-60s next year** (2027 cons 67.6% "doable"), demand visibility into **2029-30**, N3 above corp-avg profitability in 2H26. A Kaizad ex-Intel-VP expert call frames the **CoWoS constraint** (single interposer forgiving on warpage but ~2/3 of the wafer wasted, ~3-4× reticle; TSMC qualifying more OSATs + shifting to CoWoS-L) as **exactly what opens the Intel EMIB-T second-source door for Google TPUs** — demand > supply for 2.5D over 2-3 yrs. BofA's lightning round adds the pricing-power tell (**"charging an arm and a leg for new wafers"**). Backfilled pre-print: **Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (07-15)** holds TSMC top-10 on improving utilization + pricing power / high incremental-margin leverage; and the **Bernstein "CPU Renaissance"** read-through (06-17) notes Cobalt200/Graviton5/Vera/EPYC all on TSMC 3nm, its key risk being **TSMC/Intel/Samsung capacity insufficient to feed both CPU and GPU growth**. **07-21 (Outlook broker pull) — the two dedicated ratings + the tape read:** BofA (Haas Liu) BUY, **PO NT$3,100** (20x '27E), FY26/27/28 EPS lifted to **NT$110.2/153.2/182**; MS (Charlie Chan) OW, PT **raised to NT$2,988** (via recap), quantifying ~$3bn of the ~$8bn capex hike as ~5% equipment-price inflation (a bullish semicap tell) — though **JPM/Gokul reads tool-price inflation as only a *minor* factor** (the hike mostly Fab 18 P9 N3 tool pull-ins + N5→N3 conversion + new-fab cleanroom), a genuine broker divergence on *why* capex rose; Rothschild/Redburn (n/r) plays the US-$265bn/10-fabs + "no food on the table" competitive angle. On the *muted* tape the cross-broker verdict is unanimous — the clean beat-and-raise (rev guide >40%, capex $60–64bn, +$100bn US) sold off inside a broad **SOX −9.9%/5D momentum unwind** (Kimi-K3 "DeepSeek-moment" scare + systematic selling), which JPM (*Silver Linings*), UBS and Redburn frame as **positioning/valuation, not fundamentals** — a buy-the-dip setup, the only live debate still the near-term 2H margin. **07-28 adds the first genuinely two-sided packaging datapoint of the window, from the Fubon primary: CoWoS is unchanged-to-better (180kwpm end-2027 held, 220kwpm end-2028 with flagged upside) but SoIC is CUT — 4Q28 to 70k from Fubon's own 90k — because TSMC is slowing SoIC to prioritise CoWoS. That is a clean prioritisation trade rather than a demand signal, and it puts Fubon in direct conflict with Redburn (06-23, "aggressive SOIC expansion 2027-28") and Susquehanna (07-11, "SoIC ramping meaningfully through 2028"), both still on the page. The exposed name outside TSMC is BESI (hybrid bonders; TSMC its largest customer). ASE FoCoS goes the other way, raised to 50kwpm for 2027 from Fubon's own 45k, which is the offload leg of the same decision.** **08-03 (the Fubon PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT of the same franchise) closes three of the loops the 07-28 note left open and opens one new competitive one. Closed: (i) the SoIC cut is located — AP7 PHASES 2 AND 3, which were "supposedly to add SoIC capacities there" — and it has a SECOND cause beyond CoWoS priority, namely that "the CPU demand is also being pushed out"; ⚠️ that is a demand statement sitting against this page's own CPU-renaissance thread, and it should be read as timing (the CPU volume arrives later than the SoIC capex schedule assumed) rather than smoothed away. (ii) The 180kwpm-end-2027 / 220kwpm-2028 CoWoS pair is restated live and MAINTAINED, with the upside flag re-stated as an observation ("we OBSERVE TSMC is slowing down the ASIC expansion to prioritize CoWoS production"). (iii) The FY27 capex mark moves up again — to US$80-85bn from Fubon's own US$80bn (07-17) and US$75bn (07-08) — and it comes with the most useful caveat of the window: "I'M NOT SURE THAT IS ENOUGH TO SUPPORT ALL THE CUSTOMERS DEMAND," with 2nm and 3nm supply "VERY, VERY TIGHT" in 2027. A sell-side analyst doubting the sufficiency of an $80-85bn capex is the supply-side form of the ≥10%-wafer-hike thesis this page already carries, and [[AVGO]] staying at TSMC for foundry means no second source relieves it. Opened: TSMC is "working on THEIR IN-HOUSE EMIB SOLUTION… still at a VERY, VERY EARLY STAGE, no production timeline yet," and its public commentary on Intel's EMIB has become "MORE OPEN-MINDED" — Fubon's inference being that TSMC "has gone through negotiation with both Google and MediaTek." The incumbent is answering EMIB-T rather than only absorbing the overflow; too late for the 2028 [[GOOG]] TPU decision, but it caps how long Intel's window stays open. And the OSAT frame gets a sequence: ASE is a PARTNER while CoWoS is tight ("I wouldn't say TSMC is ASE's competitor… in 26 and 27"), turning competitive later via TSMC's Arizona advanced-packaging fab, where TSMC keeps CoW/CoP front-end and the on-substrate work goes to Amkor — "the most likely competitor for ASE, but NOT NOW" — precisely because ASE "has no plan to follow TSMC's footprint to build their fab in the US."** **🔴 08-20 CLOSES THE LOOP THE 08-03 CALL LEFT HALF-OPEN ON THE ASE RELATIONSHIP, AND IT DOES SO BY NAMING WHICH PACKAGING FLAVOUR GIVES FIRST.** BofA's Asia semis + components call reports that **"TSMC is running out of space for CoWoS-R packaging for CPU and is indirectly outsourcing that business to ASE."** ➜ **The page already had TSMC prioritising CoWoS over SoIC from the memory-of-record side (Fubon 07-28: SoIC 4Q28 cut to 70k from 90k; 08-03: located at AP7 phases 2-3, with "the CPU demand is also being pushed out" as a second cause). BofA supplies the CPU-side half of the same decision: it is not only that SoIC capex slipped — CoWoS-R FOR CPU is itself out of room and going to ASE. That converts Fubon's "ASE is a PARTNER while CoWoS is tight… turning competitive later" from a posture into a named, variant-specific offload, and it says the flavour that gives is CoWoS-R for CPU rather than CoWoS-L for accelerators.** ⚠️ **Both directions belong on the record: an offload is evidence of demand exceeding TSMC's own capacity (bullish for the ≥10% 2027 wafer-hike thesis and consistent with Fubon's doubt that US$80-85bn of FY27 capex is "enough to support all the customers demand") AND a datapoint on how much of the advanced-packaging value chain TSMC hands out when forced to choose.** ⚠️ **One tension left deliberately unresolved rather than smoothed: BofA says MERCHANT CPU SUPPLY IS GETTING TIGHTER INTO 2027, while Fubon (08-03) cited CPU demand being "pushed out" as a reason for the SoIC deferral. Those are different claims — supply of finished CPUs versus the timing of the packaging capex that serves them — and neither is superseded.** **Also dated in the same call: new ASIC projects are "picking up strongly from the front end manufacturing perspective and then will be through the back end packaging, the CoWoS packaging pretty soon from FOURTH QUARTER THIS YEAR" to support TPU volume doubling in 2027 — a 4Q26 CoWoS-entry date for the new ASIC cohort, consistent with the 180kwpm-end-2027 mark rather than additive to it.** **And on front-end loading, from a separate broker-hosted call the same day: VERA RUBIN CHIP-LEVEL VOLUME AT TSMC IS PUT AT ~2.0–2.1 MILLION (JPM Asia hardware/supply-chain call, 2026-08-20) — logged narrowly as a foundry-loading mark consistent with the existing CoWoS ladder, with the accelerator-level series left to [[NVDA]].** ⚠️⚠️ **That JPM file is a severe ASR defect (13,071 `[silence]` filler lines around ~20KB of real content, heavy homophone garbling), so the ~2.0–2.1m is an order-of-magnitude channel mark, not a TSMC disclosure and not a capex-model input. Neither 08-20 source revises any capacity, capex or GM number on this page.**

**🆕 Addendum 2026-08-26 (/run-inbox — 2 fontes Tier-1 + 7 de arquivo; NADA SUPERSEDIDO).** ① **A conversão CoWoS→GW é o item novo mais útil, e ela veio de investidores empurrando de volta contra a MS, não de um exhibit:** 19mn de chips implicados pelo CoWoS de 2027, a 2kW de TDP médio, exigem **~38GW** — NVIDIA 16 / AMD 9 / Google 7 / AWS 2 / MSFT 1 / Meta 1 / outros 1. ⚠️ **Travão metodológico explícito: isso é carga de TI derivada de volume de chip, não GW de facility e não custo de construção. Não encadear com o ledger de facility-GW desta wiki nem com qualquer métrica de "$/watt".** O cross-check da SpaceX é o que dá escala: **8GW incrementais ≈ 4mn Rubin ≈ ~20% de tudo que a TSMC consegue produzir em 2027.** ② **A alocação de CoWoS de 2027 mostra concentração CAINDO na margem** — NVIDIA de 56% para 45% do total, com AMD saltando de 9% para 20% e a [[MEDIATEK]] de 3% para 7%; em volume absoluto, porém, a NVIDIA sobe de 780k para 1.222k wafers. ③ **A MS de semicap coloca a TSMC em US$43,3bn de leading-logic WFE em 2027 e a [[INTC]] em US$20,5bn** — o nº2 chegando a ~metade do nº1 é novo neste ciclo e é o qualificador competitivo que registro sem adotar como tese. ④ **O bloco de arquivo DAMNANG (7 peças, março a junho) não move nenhum número vivo, e o valor dele é outro:** dá o desenho mecânico do fosso (PDK+DTCO, custo de máscara por nó, OIP, gap de capex, e o argumento de pure-play que não compete com o cliente), o roadmap de área de package (5,5× → 9,5× → 14× o reticle) e **dois vetores que cortam CONTRA a página**: (a) o outsourcing de CoWoS que está migrando de oS para processos relacionados a CoW **e que em parte é diversificação deliberada de hyperscaler, não overflow temporário**; (b) a incerteza não resolvida sobre **onde** o vidro entra no CoPoS (carrier, core substrate ou interposer) com C.C. Wei dizendo *"another two to three years and there are no shortcuts"*. ⑤ ⚠️ **NÃO TOCADO POR DECISÃO:** o conteúdo de **Intel Foundry** e o de **Apple** de "The Age of the TSMC Bottleneck" pertencem a outras páginas e não foram foldados aqui; e o arquivo "From Transistor to Chip: RTL2GDS" (2026-03-20) foi **descartado inteiro** — os 5 hits de TSMC ali são exemplos didáticos de PDK e de GAA, sem conteúdo sobre a empresa.

## Changelog
- **2026-08-26 (/run-inbox — 2 fontes Tier-1 + 7 de arquivo Tier-2). 6 linhas na seção `## Intra-quarter — Q3 FY26 window`, 1 addendum de síntese, 8 links em Sources, este registro. NADA SUPERSEDIDO — nenhum número de capacidade, capex, GM, EPS, rating ou PT da TSMC foi tocado.**
  🔴 **FONTE 1 — Morgan Stanley Asia · Charlie Chan et al., "AI Supply Chain: Google ASIC, who gets what for design services?" (2026-08-24).** O item de maior valor é a **conversão CoWoS → GW**: 19mn de chips implicados pelo CoWoS de 2027, a **2kW de TDP médio**, exigindo **~38GW** de capacidade elétrica (NVIDIA 16 / AMD 9 / Google 7 / AWS 2 / MSFT 1 / Meta 1 / outros 1), levantada por investidores em Hong Kong como uma dúvida sobre se a infraestrutura de energia sustenta o volume de chip. ⚠️ **REGRA APLICADA: isto é carga de TI derivada de volume de chip — NÃO é GW de facility e NÃO é custo de construção. Não foi encadeado com o ledger de facility-GW / IT-load-GW desta wiki e não deve ser cruzado com métricas de "$/watt", que em notas de AI-infra significam RECEITA por watt.** Também foldado: o cross-check da SpaceX (8GW incrementais ≈ 4mn Rubin ≈ ~20% da produção possível da TSMC em 2027, contra os 3GW que o Adam Jonas modela), a **alocação de CoWoS de 2027 por cliente** (NVDA 1.222k/45%, AMD 530k/20%, AVGO 484k/18%, MEDIATEK 180k/7%) e o **CAGR de receita de IA 2024-29e de ~60%**. ⚠ O item de Blackwell/Rubin da mesma nota (5,4M em 2026, ~7M em 2027, ~90k racks NVL72) **já estava** em `## Current state`, ingerido pelo /wiki-ingest — conferi e não dupliquei.

BofA supplies the CPU-side half of the SAME decision — CoWoS-R for CPU is itself out of room and going to ASE. That converts Fubon's "ASE is a PARTNER while CoWoS is tight… turning competitive later" from a posture into a NAMED, VARIANT-SPECIFIC OFFLOAD, and it says the flavour that gives is CoWoS-R for CPU rather than CoWoS-L for accelerators.** ⚠️ **Recorded two-sided: an offload is evidence of demand exceeding TSMC's own capacity (bullish for the ≥10% 2027 wafer-hike thesis and consistent with Fubon's stated doubt that US$80-85bn FY27 capex is "enough to support all the customers demand") AND a datapoint on how much advanced-packaging value TSMC hands out when forced to choose.**

  🔴 **APPLE IR SAYS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS TSMC's ADVANCED NODES: "THE PRIMARY SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS FOR APPLE'S PRODUCTS, ESPECIALLY FOR Q3 AND Q4, ARE ON THE ADVANCED NODES FOR SOCs (TSMC), NOT MEMORY COMPONENTS, CONTRARY TO SOME MARKET SPECULATION" (Apple IR — Suhasini Chandramouli, post-earnings call, 2026-08-06; Capstone's own call).** ⚠️ **LOGGED AS AN OPEN CONTRADICTION, NOT A FACT ABOUT TSMC's CAPACITY. Tim Culpan reported the day before that Apple and its assemblers are "SCRAMBLING" and rushing DRAM shipments six weeks before launch, and @zephyr_z9 relayed it as "TSMC is sitting on $1B worth of Apple processors BECAUSE THEY CAN'T GET THE DRAM TO PACKAGE THEM." The reconciliation recorded on [[AAPL]]'s page applies here: because TSMC's new WMCM process CO-PACKAGES the A20 Pro WITH DRAM, a DRAM shortage physically presents as finished SoCs stranded awaiting packaging — which IR could describe as an advanced-node/SoC constraint while the binding input is memory. If that is right, TSMC's N2 wafer output is NOT the constraint and its PACKAGING throughput is gated by a customer-supplied component. Which input binds is OPEN; the $1B stranded-WIP figure is a FinTwit relay and appears nowhere in the primary Culpan excerpt — do not cite it as sourced.**
  🔴 **THE WMCM / N2 PRODUCTION DETAIL, net-new for this page (Culpium · Tim Culpan, exclusive from Taipei, 2026-08-05): "This year's iPhone will use Apple's A20 PRO processor, THE FIRST CHIP TO BE MANUFACTURED ON TSMC's N2 NODE which started shipping in volume earlier this year. WMCM IS AN ALTERNATIVE TO INTEGRATED FAN-OUT (InFO) WHICH TSMC HAS BEEN USING FOR A DECADE. WMCM CAN BE ROUGHLY DESCRIBED AS 'CoWoS FOR MOBILE.' N2 IS PRODUCED AT FAB 20 (HSINCHU, BAOSHAN) AND FAB 22 (KAOHSIUNG), WITH THE LATTER PLANNED AS THE KEY HUB FOR APPLE CHIPS."** ⚠️ **Two things worth carrying: (1) a decade-old packaging platform (InFO) is being replaced for the flagship mobile SoC — a new advanced-packaging revenue and yield-risk line, and the "CoWoS for mobile" framing implies TSMC is extending its packaging moat from accelerators into handsets; (2) the fab assignment (N2 at Fab 20 + Fab 22, Kaohsiung as the Apple hub) is a concrete capacity-allocation datapoint. Single-journalist sourcing, credible and Taipei-based, but not company-confirmed.**

Wei's agentic-AI CPU resurgence, Fubon's own "server-CPU very strong growth 2027+", the Vera/Venice CoWoS pull). Logged as a TIMING tension and deliberately NOT netted away.** **NET-NEW #2, competitively the most interesting: TSMC IS BUILDING ITS OWN EMIB. "Based on our checks, TSMC is also working on their IN-HOUSE EMIB SOLUTION for different chip design. But currently, it is still at a VERY, VERY EARLY STAGE. And there is NO PRODUCTION TIMELINE YET." Alongside it, TSMC's public commentary on Intel's EMIB has become "MORE OPEN-MINDED," which Fubon reads as TSMC having "gone through negotiation with both Google and MediaTek" — recorded as HIS INFERENCE, not a disclosure. The read: the incumbent is answering EMIB-T rather than only absorbing the overflow into CoWoS-L/CoPoS; too late to affect the 2028 [[GOOG]] TPU decision, but it caps the DURATION of Intel's window and is now the standing counter to any thesis treating EMIB-T as a permanent second source.** **NET-NEW #3, the advanced-packaging competitive SEQUENCE, which this page had only as a static picture: ASE is a PARTNER not a competitor in 2026-27 ("I wouldn't say TSMC is ASE's competitor… because the CoWoS capacity is very very tight, so some customer just go to ASE for the production"), turning competitive "in the following years" via TSMC's Arizona advanced-packaging fab — TSMC keeps the front end (CoW/CoP) and the on-substrate work goes to Amkor, "the most likely competitor for ASE, but NOT NOW" — and the mechanism is that ASE "currently has no plan to follow TSMC's footprint to build their fab in the US." ⚠️ Attribution caveat recorded rather than hidden: the OSAT name is rendered by the ASR as "code"/"Cowd" and is read as Amkor on context (US footprint, Arizona on-substrate work), corroborated independently by UBS on this page (07-27: "ASE the primary Taiwan partner, Amkor the most likely Arizona on-substrate partner"). Nothing beyond that inference is asserted.** **Also logged, as restatements rather than new data: capacity is the binding constraint on Google's 12-15mn 2028 TPU target ("TSMC, they don't have enough capacity to do that… that's why I say Intel's is a must have in 2028" — detail on [[INTC]]/[[GOOG]]/[[MEDIATEK]]); and on the overbooking/double-booking bear, Fubon defers entirely to management's own earnings-call answer (production plan negotiated with all customers, "no overbooking, double booking," chips do not become inventory, still positive on AI demand and still raising capex). Supplier scope: domestic advanced-packaging suppliers say ASE is their fastest-growing customer.** ⚠️ **Housekeeping flag for the orchestrator: this page has NO "Sinal vs gestão" table — its Q3 FY26 intra-quarter window uses a `Post-print synthesis` paragraph instead. Per the no-invented-headers rule, the mandated re-evaluation was written INTO that paragraph rather than a new table being scaffolded. Also: the ASR caveat and the garbled-name glossary are carried inline in the 08-03 row.** **Deliberately NOT asserted: the "Seasam" supplier name is left as `[ASR-garbled Taiwanese adv-packaging supplier]` (the Jefferies desk relay of the same call renders it "C SUN," noted as plausible but not claimed), and Fubon's TSMC-negotiated-with-Google-and-MediaTek line is marked as inference.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[GOOG]], [[AVGO]], [[NVDA]], [[AMD]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the CoWoS outsourcing ratio is now quantified through 2028, and TSMC's gross-margin expansion is decomposed into price vs cost.** No TSMC rating or PT superseded (read-across plus third-party analysis). ⚠️ **The most reusable number added: the OUTSOURCED share of CoWoS goes 8% (2024) → 46% (2025) → 75% (2026E) → 85% (2027E) → 90% (2028E), with ASE's oS-outsourcing revenue $81mn → $697mn → $2,019mn → $3,809mn → $5,437mn** (JPM · Hariharan, ASE 2Q26, OW PT NT$650→NT$700, 2026-07-30). **The implication for how this page reads TSMC capacity commentary: with ~three-quarters of CoWoS already outsourced and heading to 90%, TSMC's own CoWoS capacity figures increasingly UNDERSTATE effective system capacity — CoWoS supply questions have to be answered at the TSMC+OSAT level, not TSMC alone.** ASE guided **LEAP revenue to at least DOUBLE in 2027 (implying $7bn+)** off a 2026 base already ahead of its $3.5bn guide; JPM models **~$4bn/$8bn/$12bn for 2026/27/28** and Jefferies-Fubon models **US$8.5bn in FY27, above company guidance**, with **FoCoS capacity including SPIL reaching 50k wpm by end-FY27** and ASE described as **"the most aggressive player in booking CoWoS tools"** (Shang, 2026-07-31). **Full-Process CoWoS is the 2027 driver — starting with AMD Venice this year, scaling in 2027, with possible extension to NVDA Vera or AWS Trainium depending on AMD execution; yield "progressing well."** ASE raised 2026 capex a SECOND time by $2bn to **US$10.5bn** across 20+ facility projects, and is lifting its structural ATM GM range from 24-30% to **above 30%** (Jefferies: mid-30s in FY27). ASE positioned as **"the primary partner for TSMC and the largest wafer-level packaging capacity provider outside of TSMC, much bigger than Amkor or Intel,"** with privileged access to leading-edge technologies **including COUPE**; panel-level packaging (310×310mm) ramps 1Q27, with CPO and EMIB contributing from 2027 and more meaningfully in 2028. **Separately, and materially for the margin debate: Chipstrat decomposes TSMC's recent gross-margin expansion as "gross margin rose 9.1 points, and manufacturing performance explained 3.2 of those points. The other 5.9 points? PRICING" — roughly two-thirds price, not cost, against management framing that points to utilisation and cost reduction. TSMC "historically played it safe, erring on the side of undercharging… That's changing"** (Lyons, 2026-07-31). | 2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch) | **4 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** (Fubon Buy NT$3,500, UBS Buy, Nomura Buy, BofA all stand). **The headline is that the EMIB-T threat finally got stress-tested by a house that covers both sides, and TSMC wins in the bull case for Intel.** UBS sizes the **advanced-packaging TAM at US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR**, and then hands Intel its best plausible outcome — **13% industry share / US$16bn of sales by 2030, packages for ~24mn accelerator dies, with CoPoS not reaching MP for Nvidia until 2028 and for others until 2029-30** — and TSMC *still* compounds advanced-packaging sales at a **50% CAGR 2026-30, holds 65-70% share, and takes advanced packaging to 18% of total sales in 2030E from 9% in 2026E.** In UBS's *lower* case (stronger EMIB-T adoption) that becomes **47% CAGR / 62% share / 16% of sales** — i.e. **the base-vs-bear spread is roughly 2pts of revenue mix over four years.** That is the most useful bound on packaging-disruption risk this page has, and it argues the debate is worth far less to TSMC's numbers than to Intel's. **Not smoothed over: UBS also says EMIB-T reaches MP in H2'27-28, slightly AHEAD of CoPoS in 2028, and names TSMC's own three execution risks — "CoPoS warpage, panel process uniformity, and new equipment readiness."** The compensating technical case: CoPoS offers "tighter interconnect, better signal integrity, broader integration options such as 3D stacking (and eventually, CPO)"; EMIB-T's edge is structural cost (no interposer) and scalability to ultra-large packages on large substrates. UBS's own framing of the outcome — **EMIB-T as "a meaningful second-source for selected hyperscaler ASICs" while CoPoS stays "the preferred platform for customers prioritizing the highest performance and execution reliability"** — is a share-of-mind concession, not a share-of-revenue one. **Second, and it corroborates the above from a completely different seat: an ex-ASM International senior process engineer (AlphaSense expert call, 07-25) independently concludes that TSMC's dominance holds and that the binding constraint is TOOL availability — specifically bonding tools — "not… financial or market factors,"** with demand for CoWoS and CoPoS "outpacing supply for the foreseeable future," the market **bifurcated (TSMC in complex, OSATs in mature)**, and the TSMC-NVIDIA relationship "deeply integrated… making it difficult for competitors to break in." Caveat kept on the page: it is a 2-page expert-network summary and the expert's background is front-end ALD/process integration, so it is a well-informed prior rather than a supply-chain audit. **Third — a pattern worth naming rather than a one-off: on glass-core substrates TSMC is ALSO behind Intel.** MS's checks have **Innolux + TSMC + Ibiden reaching MP only in 2H28 or 2029, versus Intel/Lens Tech with a pilot line by end-2026 and meaningful volume from 2H27** — the same ~12-18-month ordering as EMIB-T vs CoPoS. On both bridges and substrates, Intel is earlier and TSMC is betting on the higher-performance version arriving later. Glass core is directly relevant because it attacks **warpage**, one of the three CoPoS risks UBS names. **Fourth, restated because it is management's own words and was not on this page in this form: "TSMC highlighted at its 2Q26 call that back-end sits in deeper shortage than front-end, with packaging capacity so tight it now limits customer growth"** (via Jefferies) — i.e. packaging, not wafers, is the named growth constraint, which is the demand-side justification for the entire capex build above, plus **two ADDITIONAL advanced-packaging fabs announced on the call** (a positive read to [[KLAC]]'s Advanced Packaging SPC line, C26 forecast $1B / +57% y/y). Minor housekeeping note: BofA's weekend desk recap carried TSMC 2026 capex as "$58B" in a one-line summary while its own semicap section used **$60-64B from $52-56B** — the **$60-64B guide already confirmed on this page (07-16 call) stands**; the $58B is a modelled/actual figure, not the guide. Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[NVDA]], [[KLAC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md). |

(2026-06-14)**, both folded as **dated structural bullets in Debate/thesis, NOT as intra-quarter rows** — their dates fall inside the Q1 FY26 window which was **CLOSED by the 2Q26 print (07-16)**, so per the run's backfill convention they are logged as structural context rather than injected into a closed log. From the EDA primer: TSMC as **customer and gatekeeper** (Calibre-clean + PrimeTime mandated at 7/5/3nm, PDKs co-developed ~24 months pre-production), **Taiwan = 17% of Big-3 EDA revenue from <2% of global GDP, "entirely a TSMC ecosystem effect"**, the OIP Silicon-IP library 3,000 (2010) → **93,000 items (2025)**, **Fab 18 from 4 customers/8 tape-outs (2020) → 45/262 (2024)**, TSMC never built competitive internal EDA (Intel/Samsung tried and abandoned), Cadence's expanded TSMC N2/A16 collaboration, and the outside-view risk line *"ASML faces High-NA adoption risk, and TSMC faces geopolitical concentration"* — an independent restatement of the Taiwan-concentration bear already on the page (Barron's "80% of long-term assets still in Taiwan"). From the SMIC teardown: **TSMC N6 is the measured baseline** and **SMIC N+3 "reaches TSMC N6-class logic density… [but] does not match N6 on process maturity or cost"**; N6 fin pitch measured **34nm** with density from **DTCO not tighter pitches**, HD cell 240nm, no COAG, M0/M2/M3 ~40nm on SADP with **no quadruple patterning needed anywhere** (the cost/maturity moat, quantified); TSMC used the reduced p-to-n isolation booster on N3; a theoretical SMIC N+4 reaches only N5-class density; and the historical note that **Huawei was TSMC's largest customer pre-controls — the sole customer on N7+, TSMC's first EUV node, and among the first on N5 alongside Apple — ending in late 2020**. Cross-filed [[SMIC]], [[INTC]], [[ASML]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[AAPL]]. |
| 2026-07-24 | **/wiki-ingest — 2 log rows on the Intel-print read-across; additive, no rating/PT/estimate superseded.** **BofA · Haas Liu, "Intel read through: Limited competition and supply disruption to TSMC ecosystem" (07-24)** — the competitive verdict on Intel's Q2'26 beat, the **14A HVM-2028 commitment** and the capex raise to **>$20B (CY27 "significantly above")** is that the read-across to TSMC is **limited on both competition and supply**. Logged as the necessary counterweight to the same-day bull-side Intel headlines: the capex raise is unambiguously positive for **[[ASML]] / semicap** (MS revised Intel Low-NA EUV demand from **1 to 2-4 tools this year**), but that is a **supplier** read-through, not a **share** read-through — and on share the existing page framing holds (**TSMC ~90-95% of 2nm in the first two product waves 2028-29, Intel+Samsung combined ~5-6%**, JPM · Gokul 07-08; TSMC cheaper on a yield/PPA-adjusted basis even at a higher sticker). Where Intel *is* landing is **advanced packaging (EMIB-T)**, which TSMC has publicly framed as welcome complementary capacity given CoWoS tightness — **overflow, not displacement**. Tracked as unresolved: **UBS's 07-24 checks now put MediaTek among likely EMIB-T customers for its v9-gen TPU** — a third independent corroboration (after JPM 07-01 and UBS/Sunny Lin 07-01; see [[MEDIATEK]], [[INTC]]) — against **Fubon's 07-08** read that the **95%+ yield bar Google set is "very challenging" by mid-2027**, implying a fallback to TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion. **Austin Lyons / Chipstrat (07-24), "TSMC could spend $10B more on N2 capacity. Why won't they?"** — an independent framing of the capital-discipline question central to this page: with N2 demand plainly exceeding supply (super-hot-run mix at **20-30% from 10-20%**, each tier ~+50% ASP), the binding constraint is **TSMC's own choice, not the order book**. It cuts both ways on two live threads here — it strengthens the **capex-conservatism** assumption SemiAnalysis uses to argue **~50k+ WSPM of N3 overflow could flow to Intel** ([[INTC]]), and it makes the **70% GM bridge** in the house sensitivity work easier at the same time as it enlarges the overflow-to-competitors risk. Framing piece, no new company data. Edits: 2 new Q3'26-window Full-log rows (both 07-24). |
